d:["$","article",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-[68ch] px-6 py-16","children":[["$","header",null,{"children":[["$","p",null,{"className":"font-mono text-sm tracking-widest text-muted ","children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"text-accent","children":"·"}]," ","#control theory  #sliding mode control  #predefined-time control  #paper notes"]}],["$","h1",null,{"className":"mt-4 font-serif text-4xl font-bold leading-tight md:text-5xl","children":"Booking the Settling Time with Delayed Feedback: Notes on Deng et al. (2024)"}],["$","p",null,{"className":"mt-4 font-mono text-sm text-muted","children":"Oct 12, 2026"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"className":"mt-10","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"prose prose-neutral max-w-none article-body","children":[["$","blockquote","blockquote-0",{"children":["\n",["$","p","p-0",{"children":["Yang Deng, Emmanuel Moulay, Vincent Léchappé, Zhang Chen, Bin Liang, Franck Plestan. ",["$","em","em-0",{"children":"Robust Nonsingular Predefined-Time Terminal Sliding Mode Control for Perturbed Chains of Integrators"}],". IEEE Transactions on Automatic Control, 69(12): 8946–8953, 2024. DOI: ",["$","a","a-0",{"href":"https://doi.org/10.1109/TAC.2024.3426554","children":"10.1109/TAC.2024.3426554"}],"; author copy at ",["$","a","a-1",{"href":"https://hal.science/hal-04637935","children":"hal.science/hal-04637935"}],". Affiliations: Department of Automation, Tsinghua University (Deng, Chen, Liang; Chen corresponding), XLIM, Université de Poitiers (Moulay), Ampère, INSA Lyon (Léchappé), LS2N, Nantes Université (Plestan). Supported by NSFC grants 62203252 and 62073183."]}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","h2","h2-0",{"children":"1. What the paper is after"}],"\n",["$","p","p-0",{"children":"Terminal sliding mode control (TSMC) is usually credited with two things: invariance to matched disturbances once on the sliding surface, and finite-time convergence. The field has moved past \"finite\" to fixed-time: a settling-time bound independent of initial conditions (Polyakov, 2012). But fixed-time TSMC carries an old defect, singularity: on classical terminal sliding surfaces the control blows up as the state approaches the origin. The existing nonsingular fixed-time TSMC literature (a long line starting with Zuo, 2015) has two common weaknesses:"}],"\n",["$","ol","ol-0",{"children":["\n",["$","li","li-0",{"children":"singularity is handled by switching, either between sliding surfaces or between control laws;"}],"\n",["$","li","li-1",{"children":"almost all of it covers double integrators or second-order systems only; high-order chains of integrators are left out."}],"\n"]}],"\n",["$","p","p-1",{"children":"Pal et al. (2022) and Chen et al. (2022) used time scaling and variable gains to avoid switching altogether, delivering nonsingular predefined-time TSMC, but only for matched disturbances. A separate line is the smooth periodic delayed feedback of Zhou, Michiels and Chen (2021): artificial delays buy fixed-time convergence in a smooth form, at the cost of the robustness defect stated in their Remark 4, namely that controllers built that way do not preserve robust predefined-time stability under perturbations or model uncertainties."}],"\n",["$","p","p-2",{"children":["This paper welds the two lines together: keep the ",["$","em","em-0",{"children":"form"}]," of delayed feedback for the sliding surface, and leave robustness to sliding mode theory. The resulting control law is nonsingular and switching-free; it covers matched disturbances and mismatched ones; and it extends from double integrators all the way to n-th order chains of integrators."]}],"\n","$L17","\n","$L18","\n","$L19","\n","$L1a","\n","$L1b","\n","$L1c","\n","$L1d","\n","$L1e","\n","$L1f","\n","$L20","\n","$L21","\n","$L22","\n","$L23","\n","$L24","\n","$L25","\n","$L26","\n","$L27","\n","$L28","\n","$L29","\n","$L2a","\n","$L2b","\n","$L2c","\n","$L2d","\n","$L2e","\n","$L2f","\n","$L30","\n","$L31","\n","$L32","\n","$L33","\n","$L34","\n","$L35","\n","$L36","\n","$L37","\n","$L38","\n","$L39","\n","$L3a","\n","$L3b","\n","$L3c","\n","$L3d","\n","$L3e","\n","$L3f","\n","$L40","\n","$L41","\n","$L42","\n","$L43","\n","$L44","\n","$L45","\n","$L46","\n","$L47","\n","$L48","\n","$L49","\n","$L4a","\n","$L4b","\n","$L4c","\n","$L4d","\n","$L4e","\n","$L4f","\n","$L50","\n","$L51","\n","$L52","\n","$L53"]}]}],"$L54","$L55"]}]

10e:I[2619,["2619","static/chunks/2619-3c9e02e22d10480a.js","5421","static/chunks/app/(en)/en/research/%5Bslug%5D/page-18ee6bb99c79060d.js"],""]
17:["$","p","p-3",{"children":"Terminology first. Predefined-time means the user picks the settling instant T_c up front and tunes the controller for it; convergence happens no later than T_c. Fixed-time only promises a bound independent of initial conditions, indirectly set by parameters. The paper also uses a stricter notion, all nonzero initial conditions converging exactly at T_c, which it calls prescribed-time; I keep the English."}]
